The test_export.py suggests there are tests for export with totals. However, the current export.py function doesn't support totals parameter. The test `test_export_appends_totals_row` expects `export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True)` to return a string with a totals row. But the current export_csv function only takes `rows` argument.\n\nSo the issue: export is missing totals row because the function doesn't handle adding totals row. We need to modify export.py to optionally append a totals row when some flag is set. Perhaps the function should accept an optional `totals` parameter default False, and if True, append the result of column_totals(rows) as a row with \"TOTAL\" or something.\n\nLooking at test_render_marks_last_row_has `mark_last=True` and returns [\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"] which seems odd: \"TOTAL,3,4\" where 3 and 4 are the totals? Actually column totals of [[1,2],[3,4]] would be [4,6]. But the test shows \"TOTAL,3,4\". That seems like maybe they mark the last row as total with some other logic. Hmm.\n\nBut test_export_appends_totals_row expects: \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\". So the totals row is \"TOTAL,4,6\". So the export should append a row starting with \"TOTAL\" followed by the column totals.\n\nThus we need to modify export_csv to handle a `totals` parameter (or maybe keyword argument). Currently it's defined as `def export_csv(rows):`. We need to change it to accept an optional parameter, maybe `totals=False`, and if True, compute totals and append.\n\nAlso need to import column_totals from totals module.\n\nLet's check if render_rows is used elsewhere. The export_csv calls render_rows(rows). If we want to append a totals row, we could either let render_rows handle it if we pass an extra row, or modify export_csv to add the totals row after rendering.\n\nLooking at test_render_marks_last_row_has: `render_rows([[1, 2], [3, 4]], mark_last=True) == [\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]`. This suggests render_rows can optionally mark the last row as total, but not append a separate totals row. However, the export test expects a separate totals row.\n\nMaybe the intended design is that export_csv should call render_rows on the rows, and if totals=True, also render the totals row separately using render_rows([totals_row]) or something.\n\nBut given the tests, we need to make export_csv pass the test `test_export_appends_totals_row`. Currently it fails because it doesn't include totals row.\n\nLet's look at the test file again: It has `test_export_without_totals` which tests the current behavior (without totals). `test_export_appends_totals_row` expects the new behavior with totals=True.\n\nSo we need to modify export.py to support the `totals` parameter and include the totals row when requested.\n\nLet's edit export.py accordingly.\n\nWe'll need to import column_totals from totals.\n\nWe'll change the function to:\n\n```python\nfrom render import render_rows\nfrom totals import column_totals\n\ndef export_csv(rows, totals=False):\n    lines = render_rows(rows)\n    if totals:\n        total_row = column_totals(rows)\n        # Maybe format the total row with \"TOTAL\" as first cell?\n        # The test expects \"TOTAL,4,6\" for rows [[1,2],[3,4]].\n        # So we need to prepend \"TOTAL\" to the total_row list.\n        total_row_formatted = [\"TOTAL\"] + total_row\n        lines.append(render_rows([total_row_formatted])[0])  # render_rows returns list of strings, each row rendered.\n        # Or we could do: lines.append(\",\".join(str(v) for v in [\"TOTAL\"] + total_row))\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n```\n\nBut we need to check render_rows behavior: It takes a list of rows and returns list of strings, each row is comma-joined values. So if we pass `[total_row_formatted]` as a row list, render_rows will return a list with one string which is the comma-joined row. So we can do:\n\n```python\nif totals:\n    total_row = [\"TOTAL\"] + column_totals(rows)\n    lines.extend(render_rows([total_row]))\n```\n\nBut render_rows returns list of strings for each row. So `render_rows([total_row])` returns `[\",\".join(str(v) for v in total_row)]`. So we can just do `lines.append(render_rows([total_row])[0])` or `lines += render_rows([total_row])`.\n\n simpler: `lines += render_rows([[\"TOTAL\"] + column_totals(rows)])`\n\nBut column_totals returns list of numbers (ints). We need to convert to strings? render_rows converts each value to str via `str(v)`. So it's fine.\n\nThus we can edit export.py.\n\nBut before editing, let's see if there are any other files that might be affected. The test_export.py likely needs to be updated to test the new functionality, but it already has a test for `test_export_appends_totals_row` that expects the new behavior. However, that test imports export_csv and calls it with `totals=True`. Currently export_csv doesn't have that parameter, so the test would fail with TypeError. But maybe the test is not yet run? Or the test is written against the current code? Actually the test `test_export_appends_totals_row` is present in test_export.py. It expects `export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True)` to return `\"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"`. If export_csv doesn't accept `totals` parameter, calling it with two arguments would raise TypeError. So the test might be failing already, or maybe the code has been updated partially? Let's check the current export.py again: it only has `def export_csv(rows):`. So the test would fail.\n\nBut the user says \"the export is missing the totals row\". This suggests that when exporting, the totals row is not included. This suggests that for columns where all values are non-numeric, the total should be empty string? Or maybe it should skip non-numeric and if no numeric, return empty string. The sum of numeric values in column 0: \"a\" and \"b\" are strings, no numeric, so total is empty string. Column 1: 1 and 2 are numeric, sum is 3. So we need to modify `column_totals` to check if the values are numeric before summing, and if not, put an empty string or something.\n\nBut the function currently does `[sum(r[i] for r in rows) for i in range(len(rows[0]))]`. This will fail if any r[i] is not a number. We need to change it to handle mixed types.\n\n2. `render.py`: `render_rows` function is called without `mark_last` parameter in the current code. The test `test_render_marks_last_row_as_total` expects `render_rows` to accept `mark_last=True` and return `[\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]` for input `[[1, 2], [3, 4]]`. This suggests that `render_rows` should have an optional parameter `mark_last` that, if True, marks the last row by prepending \"TOTAL\" to it? But the expected output for the last row is \"TOTAL,3,4\", where 3,4 are the last row's values [3,4]. So it prepends \"TOTAL\" to the last row's values.\n\nBut our `export_csv` uses `render_rows(rows)` without any parameters. If we want to support totals row appended, we might not need `render_rows` to mark the last row. We'll modify `render_rows` to accept an optional `mark_last=False` parameter. The test expects `[\"\", 3]` for `[[\"a\", 1], [\"b\", 2]]`. So we should compute sum only for numeric values, and if a column has no numeric values, return an empty string or 0? The test expects empty string for first column. So we can do:\n\n```python\ndef column_totals(rows):\n    if not rows:\n        return []\n    result = []\n    for i in range(len(rows[0])):\n        total = 0\n        has_numeric = False\n        for r in rows:\n            val = r[i]\n            if isinstance(val, (int, float)):\n                total += val\n                has_numeric = True\n            # else ignore non-numeric\n        if has_numeric:\n            result.append(total)\n        else:\n            result.append(\"\")\n    return result\n```\n\nBut the test expects `[\"\", 3]`. Our logic would produce `[\"\" , 3]` if we append empty string for no numeric, and 3 for numeric column. That matches.\n\nAlternatively, we could return `None` but test expects empty string.\n\nNow `export.py`: We need to modify `export_csv` to accept `totals=False` parameter, and if True, append a row consisting of \"TOTAL\" followed by the column totals (computed by `column_totals(rows)`). But note: `column_totals` now returns a list where non-numeric columns are empty strings. How should we format the totals row? The test `test_export_appends_totals_row` expects `export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True) == \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"`. Here column totals are [4,6], both numeric, so the row is \"TOTAL,4,6\". If a column has no numeric values, what should the totals row show? Maybe empty string or \"TOTAL,\" followed by empty strings? Not sure. But we can handle it by converting each total to string, and if it's empty string, it will be just empty before comma? Actually `[\"TOTAL\", \"\"]` would become \"TOTAL,\". That might be acceptable. Or we could skip empty totals? But let's follow the simplest: prepend \"TOTAL\" and then join the totals with commas. If a total is empty string, it will appear as empty field.\n\nSo in `export_csv`:\n```python\nfrom render import render_rows\nfrom totals import column_totals\n\ndef export_csv(rows, totals=False):\n    lines = render_rows(rows)\n    if totals:\n       totals_row = [\"TOTAL\"] + column_totals(rows)\n        lines.append(render_rows([totals_row])[0])  # render_rows returns list of strings, we need the first (and only) element\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n```\n\nBut note: `render_rows([totals_row])` will render the totals row as a comma-separated string. However, if `totals_row` has empty strings, it will produce empty fields.\n\nAlternatively, we can do: `lines.append(\",\".join(str(v) for v in [\"TOTAL\"] + column_totals(rows)))`. But using `render_rows` keeps consistency.\n\nBut `render_rows` now expects `mark_last` parameter optionally. We don't need that here.\n\nSo we can call `render_rows([totals_row])` which will use the default `mark_last=False`.\n\nNow, we need to update the imports: `export_csv` currently only imports `render_rows` from `render`. This will fail if any r[i] is not a number. We need to change it to handle mixed types.\n\n2. `render.py`: `render_rows` function is called without `mark_last` parameter in the current code. The test `test_render_marks_last_row_as_total` expects `render_rows` to accept `mark_last=True` and return `[\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]` for input `[[1, 2], [3, 4]]`. This suggests that `render_rows` should have an optional parameter `mark_last` that, if True, marks the last row by prepending \"TOTAL\" to it? But the expected output for the last row is \"TOTAL,3,4\", where 3,4 are the last row's values [3,4]. So it prepends \"TOTAL\" to the last row's values.\n\nBut our `export_csv` uses `render_rows(rows)` without any parameters. If we want to support totals row appended, we might not need `render_rows` to mark the last row. We can handle the totals row separately.\n\nHowever, the test `test_render_marks_last_row_as_total` exists and expects `render_rows` to support `mark_last`. So we should also fix `render_rows` to support that parameter, even if it's not currently used.\n\n3. `export.py`: `export_csv` function needs to support `totals=True` parameter and append the totals row.\n\nGiven the complexity, we should fix each function to meet the test expectations.\n\nLet's start with `render.py`. We'll modify `render_rows` to accept an optional `mark_last=False` parameter. If `mark_last` is True, then for the last row, we should prepend \"TOTAL\" to the rendered values.\n\nCurrent `render_rows`:\n```python\ndef render_rows(rows):\n    return [\",\".join(str(v) for v in r) for r in rows]\n```\n\nWe can change it to:\n```python\ndef render_rows(rows, mark_last=False):\n    rendered = [\",\".join(str(v) for v in r) for r in rows]\n    if mark_last and rows:\n        last_row = rows[-1]\n        last_rendered = \",\".join(str(v) for v in last_row)\n        rendered[-1] = \"TOTAL,\" + last_rendered\n    return rendered\n```\n\nThis would satisfy the test: for `[[1, 2], [3, 4]]`, rendered rows would be `[\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]`.\n\nNow `totals.py`: We need to handle non-numeric values. The test expects `[\"\", 3]` for `[[\"a\", 1], [\"b\", 2]]`. So we should compute sum only for numeric values, and if a column has no numeric values, return an empty string or 0? The test expects empty string for first column. So we can do:\n\n```python\ndef column_totals(rows):\n    if not rows:\n        return []\n    result = []\n    for i in range(len(rows[0])):\n        total = 0\n        has_numeric = False\n        for r in rows:\n            val = r[i]\n            if isinstance(val, (int, float)):\n                total += val\n                has_numeric = True\n            # else ignore non-numeric\n        if has_numeric:\n            result.append(total)\n        else:\n            result.append(\"\")\n    return result\n```\n\nBut the test expects `[\"\", 3]`. Our logic would produce `[\"\" , 3]` if we append empty string for no numeric, and 3 for numeric column. That matches.\n\nAlternatively, we could return `None` but test expects empty string.\n\nNow `export.py`: We need to modify `export_csv` to accept `totals=False` parameter, and if True, append a row consisting of \"TOTAL\" followed by the column totals (computed by `column_totals(rows)`). But note: `column_totals` now returns a list where non-numeric columns are empty strings. How should we format the totals row? The test `test_export_appends_totals_row` expects `export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True) == \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"`. Here column totals are [4,6], both numeric, so the row is \"TOTAL,4,6\". If a column has no numeric values, what should the totals row show? Maybe empty string or \"TOTAL,\" followed by empty strings? Not sure. But we can handle it by converting each total to string, and if it's empty string, it will be just empty before comma? Actually `[\"TOTAL\", \"\"]` would become \"TOTAL,\". That might be acceptable. Or we could skip empty totals? But let's follow the simplest: prepend \"TOTAL\" and then join the totals with commas. If a total is empty string, it will appear as empty field.\n\nSo in `export_csv`:\n```python\nfrom render import render_rows\nfrom totals import column_totals\n\ndef export_csv(rows, totals=False):\n    lines = render_rows(rows)\n    if totals:\n       totals_row = [\"TOTAL\"] + column_totals(rows)\n        lines.append(render_rows([totals_row])[0])  # render_rows returns list of strings, we need the first (and only) element\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n```\n\nBut note: `render_rows([totals_row])` will render the totals row as a comma-separated string.
